# rMBP ledger — every finding, ticked once

> Arch ledger for the 2012 rMBP (x86_64) track. Cross-arch and shared-seam items live in
> `docs/dev/LEDGER.md` (ids `S<n>`, `P<n>`); rows below cross-reference it by id.

> Rule (`docs/dev/LAWS.md` §Ledgers, Peter 2026-09-05): **an arc that fixes, flies, or drops a ledger
> item ticks it here in the same commit.** Every audit, inventory, or capture review folds its findings
> into this table the turn it lands. Before spawning any audit, brief it with this file: report only
> what is NEW or CHANGED. A finding re-derived because it was not here is the waste.

Sources (all in git): `F6` = `docs/dev/evidence/rmbp11/FLIGHT6-POSTMORTEM.md` · `F7` = `docs/dev/evidence/rmbp11/FLIGHT7-POSTMORTEM.md` ·
`LR` = `docs/dev/evidence/rmbp11/LANDING-REPORT.md` · `PR` = `docs/dev/evidence/rmbp10/PANELREFUSE-REVIEW.md` ·
`B11`/`B12` = batons `rmbp-11.md` / `rmbp-12.md` (seat-local; ids only) · MANIFEST = the bench media record (seat-local).
Columns: `owner` = the lane whose file changes (orin · pi · rmbp · shared-gate); `flies-on` = the bench that scores it.
Status enum (GATE-LEDGER): **open** · **fixed-unflown** · **flown** · **landed** · **dropped** — free text after " — ".

| A1 | BAR1 wedge under paint bursts (`storm`): a core dies mid-blit; REHOME + shell re-mint recover the desktop on the remaining cores, the wedged core stays dead for the boot | rmbp | rmbp | open — **THE PAIR FLEW 2026-09-16 (flights 8 WC + 9 UC, one storm each) AND THE APERTURE THEORY IS FALSIFIED.** Both legs crossed the tripwire — `[wcser] PASS OVERDUE holder=c1 … blit_inflight=1` 16x on WC, 12x on UC — so §11.3's `aperture=uc` and the wedge happens anyway row fired: **W4 EXONERATED**, the panel aperture's memory type leaves the ladder, and W5 (credits / the GPU window path) is the head with nothing above it. `d_lnksta=0000 d_devsta=0000 secsta=2000 d_secsta=2000 lnkdis=0` on all 28 samples of both boots, so **W2's shut-out is CONFIRMED** on an instrument that can tell the instant from the boot. `relatch=secsta:2000` on both legs: **enumeration on this machine DOES latch Received Master Abort**, measured for the first time, so boots 8/9/11's `secsta=2000` is explained without the wedge and W7's residue reading dies on evidence. `d_secsta=2000` at `n=1` does NOT yet reach the wedge — see B113, the named `wifi/bus.rs:125` alternative is LIVE on these two boots. `cto value=50us-50ms(default) dis=0 capver=2 v2=1` — the prober is bounded at 50 ms and W6 is answerable here. **Neither machine died**: presents continued, no `PANIC`, no `REHOMED`, no `DEAD c<n>` on either boot; the UC leg's cost is A13. Convicted, unfixed; the recovery path is metal-proven (F6 boot 1: c1 at 224 s, `GATE STOLEN … REHOMED … DEAD c1`). **RUNGS STAGED FOR FLIGHT, not a fix: BAR1WEDGE (P6) + SECSTA2 (P7)** — shut-out register §6 rungs P6 and P7, both behind the ONE knob `UNAOS_BAR1WEDGE=1` (P7 added no knob), default OFF, neither flown. It is the falsifier the ONE coded never-flown experiment for this row (P5, `UNAOS_BAR1EXP=uc`) needs to be scorable: a UC boot alone yields one bit that the ~6.8x UC slowdown explains as readily as the theory does. Fly the pair `UNAOS_BAR1EXP=uc UNAOS_BAR1WEDGE=1`, with a `UNAOS_BAR1WEDGE=1` WC boot as the control. Two findings landed with it, both about evidence rather than hardware: LNKSTA[15:14] are RW1C latches nobody had cleared and BOTH are set in the `lnksta=d081` this row cites, and the root port's completion-timeout configuration had never been read at all. P7 (SECSTA2) adds the SECOND W1C clear, taken after the last bus walk of the boot, so P6's `d_secsta`/`d_lnksta` at the first stall are deltas against the end of enumeration; its `relatch=` field measures which RW1C bits enumeration itself sets — the premise the "`secsta=2000` is residue" reading rests on and which nothing had ever measured. A third finding landed with it, again about evidence: the residual P6 recorded named the wrong walk — `ehci::init` (`arch/x86_64/pci.rs:838`) runs BEFORE the at-arm clear (`arch/x86_64/pci.rs:1016`), so the EHCI enumeration was never the leak **P7 IS NOW COMPLETE: DEVSTA (2026-09-15, `exec-rmbp-smallfix4`, parent `1c7c1733` — re-derive the sha at the land) closed the leftover SECSTA2 named in its own text** ("closing it is two lines here, read `cap + 0x0A`, clear `DEVSTA_W1C`, store it — left to the seat rather than taken silently"). Device Status is now read, RW1C-cleared on the same CF8 write path and re-baselined with its two neighbours, so the `wedge-sample` line's `d_lnksta`/`d_secsta`/`d_devsta` **all** delta against the end of enumeration instead of two against one window and the third against `pci::init`'s Kepler dispatch; the post-enum line carries `devsta=<before>-><after>` and `relatch=… devsta:<bits>`. The VALUE is unchanged expectation (`devsta=0000` on every capture there has ever been); what is fixed is a READING HAZARD — three deltas printed on one line read as one measurement, and one of them was not. A `d_devsta=0004` (Unsupported Request Detected, the likeliest bit for a read of a wedged BAR) could have been latched by any of the three post-at-arm enumeration walks with nothing on the wire to say so. Still no new knob, no new call site, and no QEMU evidence is possible — q35 has no GK107, so `PCIH_READY` stays false and the line is honestly absent; `./arroyo check` is the only gate this rung can have off metal. Three scoring rows for `relatch=devsta:` are added to PCIE-RP-RECOVERY.md §11.3, so the flight scores it rather than reading it. **CORRECTION 2026-09-16 (STEALMS, branch `exec-rmbp-stealms`, parent `5ebe29f4`; tip sha at the fold — a commit cannot name its own hash): the sentence above, "no `REHOMED`, no `DEAD c<n>` on either boot", is FALSE on the capture this row cites.** Measured: `LC_ALL=C grep -a -c -F 'REHOMED the render role' capture/rmbp12-flight8/ttyUSB0.log` = 7 and `LC_ALL=C grep -a -c -F 'GATE STOLEN' capture/rmbp12-flight8/ttyUSB0.log` = 7 (control `LC_ALL=C grep -a -c -F 'ZZZNOTPRESENTZZZ'` = 0; four on flight 8, three on flight 9), every steal `after 4016-4303ms`, `revenants=0` throughout: both machines survived by LOSING a core per stall, not by not stalling — flight 8 lost c1, c2, c3, c4 in 63 s and ran render on c5. In the same commit the steal bound `COMP_GATE_STEAL_MS` (`video/wm.rs:9496`) is lowered 4_000 -> 1_500 ms (5x the 302 ms record honest pass, 1.5x `COMP_PASS_OVERDUE_MS`; B116): each freeze becomes ~1.5 s instead of ~4 s, the core is still lost, W5 stays the repair; the next stall must print `GATE STOLEN … after 15xx ms`, never `4xxx`. | F6; `[pcih] rp-at-wedge lnksta=d081`; the wedge-theory ladder, the field-by-field decode and the flight score card: `docs/dev/OS/08_VIDEO/PCIE-RP-RECOVERY.md` §11 (P7: §11.4); the rungs' register rows: `docs/dev/OS/08_VIDEO/SHUTOUT-REGISTER.md` §6 P6 and P7 | — |

| A3 | `reboot` verb resets the machine (FADT RESET_REG 0xcf9 ← 0x6) but NONE of the ladder's witnesses reach the console: `raw_write_str` is the 16550 at 0x3F8, which this laptop lacks; the reset lands before the xHCI pass drains the FTDI mirror ring | rmbp | rmbp | flown — **CLOSED ON METAL 2026-09-16 (flight 8, `reboot` typed over the wire).** The last four lines of the boot, in order and as the final bytes before the reset: `[pwrreboot] reboot verb invoked — dispatching the platform mechanism`, `[pwrreboot] x86 mechanism: FADT RESET_REG ladder (acpi_power::reboot)`, `[pwrreboot] ring drained lines=0 bytes=0 mirror=ok`, `[pwrreboot] ftdi flushed bytes=7503 transfers=118 exhausted=0`; flight 9 the same shape at `bytes=436 transfers=24`. **⚠ THE RESIDUAL BELOW IS DISCHARGED AND THE PLAYBOOK'S KNOWN LIST WAS WRONG:** `[pwrreboot] ring drained …` DID reach the cable, carrying `mirror=ok` — SINKDRAIN routed `power_drain` through `_print`'s sink set, and the pre-registered expectation that it would never reach the cable is corrected by this capture. RBTDRAIN on `exec-rmbp-rbtdrain` (the sha is the commit carrying this row; a self-referencing sha cannot be written into its own commit, so the seat folds it at the land): the design this row asked for, built. `xhci::ftdi_flush_sync(budget_cycles) -> (bytes, transfers, exhausted)` pumps `drain_ftdi` until the FTDI mirror ring is empty or the budget is spent, takes the controller with `claim()` (the WEDGE-8 loan — answers `Busy`, never waits: the LOCKFIX path this row names) and retries only INSIDE the budget, so a wedged pass costs the reboot its budget and never the machine. Two call sites and neither is redundant: `power.rs` calls it after `power_drain` on BOTH x86 verbs and then writes `[pwrreboot] ftdi flushed bytes=… transfers=… exhausted=0 (or 1)` into the ring, and `acpi_power::reboot`'s FIRST statement (line-neutral fold on the signature, as S5DRAIN's is on `poweroff`'s) is the same call — which is what carries that line to the cable, and is also the last point before `interrupts::disable()` at which the TX pump's `hlt()` can still wake. UNGATED: transport correctness, the same standing `power_drain` and `s5_ring_flush` have; NO new knob. `drain_ftdi` gained a bool return (true = nothing left to push, false = PTBURST slice yield) for this and nothing else. QEMU-green, UNFLOWN on metal (no rMBP boot since). BOTH x86 power ports carry the fold: `acpi_power::poweroff`'s signature line is `s5_ring_flush(); ftdi_flush_sync(…)` in that order (S5DRAIN's 'first statement' property unmoved), so `video/crystal.rs`'s Shut Down and `video/instgui.rs`, which call `poweroff` DIRECTLY, reach the cable too. ⚠ RESIDUAL, found by this fixture and NOT closed here: `[pwrreboot] ring drained lines=N bytes=M` is on serial.log and NOT on the cable — `serial_ring::power_drain` writes the drained lines AND its tally through `arch::serial::raw_write_str`, the 16550 alone, never through `_print`, so on this laptop PWRDRAIN empties the staging ring into a port that is not there and a line DEFERRED under contention is consumed before any FTDI flush can see it. What RBTDRAIN carries is everything `_print` reached the mirror ring with. Shared-file fix (`serial_ring.rs`): trunk `docs/dev/QUEUE.md` §5 — **and it is no longer the gap this row feared; SINKDRAIN closed it and flight 8 proves it at the cable** | F6 (zero `[orinreboot]` lines, 3 boots); F7 (`value=0x6`); `docs/dev/OS/02_KERNEL_CORE/serial_transport.md` §RBTDRAIN (mechanism, the two call sites, the fixture and its go-red); docs/dev/evidence/rmbp-0916/flight8-9/FLIGHT8-9.md §4 (the four lines verbatim) | flights 8 and 9, 2026-09-16 |

| A5 | Shell window tears under storm: `[wc-h] win=2 torn=111 banded=13085` while the eight vug windows sit at torn 3–10; `[wcser] declined_pct=51 -> SERIAL`, `[wc-w] amp=1.28x -> WIDENED` | rmbp | rmbp | **flown** — 2026-09-16, flights 8 and 9: THE BEAM SOURCE WORKS AND THE ROW IS CONVERTED, NOT CLOSED, exactly as its own closing clause pre-registered.** `:: BEAMX86: head=0 vtotal=1852 samples=22025 vblank_delta=2 -> ARMED ::` on both boots, and every `[wc-h] rollup` now carries `beam=obs` with `beamgiveup=0`. **The baseline was measured, not assumed: flight 7's capture contains ZERO lines carrying `beam=` and its highest `torn=` anywhere is 4** — the counter was silent while the panel tore, which is the defect BEAMX86 was cut for; A5's `torn=111` is F6's number and the playbook's attribution of it to flight 7 is corrected. Flight 8 (WC): Shell `win=2 torn=602` of 7437 bracketed presents, `maxpresent_us=11630 presspread=50 longpres=0 -> AT-RISK`; the six storm vugs total **3 torn presents in 2201**. Flight 9 (UC): `STAT win=3 torn=935` of 990 presents (94%), Shell `torn=196` with `longpres=20` and `maxpresent_us=69120`, vug `win=8 torn=127`. **THE OPEN QUESTION, and this flight does not choose between the three readings:** Peter saw vug tearing on flight 8 (*"there was tearing in vug"*) where the counter reads 3/2201 — either his word names the desktop region rather than the six storm windows, or the bracket is right and they did not tear, or the tear class the eye sees there is not the class `beamcross` counts. Per-window table: docs/dev/evidence/rmbp-0916/flight8-9/FLIGHT8-9.md §6. BEAMX86, folded (`exec-rmbp-beamx86`): the x86 beam SOURCE, which is the one thing `video/beam.rs` was missing here.** The tearing FIX has been written since orin 26 and folds to a no-op without a raster position; `arch/x86_64/mod.rs::scanout_beam` was a constant `None`, so on this board every present was unbracketed and `torn=` stayed the DURATION predicate — the same instrument that read 0 on the Orin while the panel tore (TEAR-DIAG). Now: `drivers/gpu/kepler_display.rs::beam_probe`, appended at the file tail behind `all(nvidia-kepler, beam)` and called from ONE folded statement at the end of `takeover_display` (after `panel_console_resume`, before the `wc` activation), samples `HEAD_STAT.VERT` (`NV_PDISPLAY_BASE + 0x6000 + head*0x800 + 0x340`; `vline[15:0]`, `vblank_count[31:16]`; rnndb g80_pdisplay.xml:647, GK104 stride 0x800) on all four heads for 45 ms each and ARMS the first whose line counter CLIMBS and whose `vblank_count` ticks twice, with `vtotal = max(vline) + 1`. READ-ONLY, bounded by both a time budget and a spin cap. **`vtotal` is SAMPLED, not read: rnndb cites no lines-per-frame register in this bank, and the only raster-shaped word this module knows of sits at an offset found by VALUE match, not citation** — the head's EVO `SIZE` readback rides the same witness line for cross-check (`vtotal >= the matching half`, by the vblank rows; never equality). Because the probe is bolted INSIDE `takeover_display`, past all five of its early returns, it cannot arm on a boot where the Kepler takeover declined — which is also why q35 shows zero `BEAMX86` lines with the code demonstrably in the artifact. Knob-off holds both ways: `./arroyo knoboff beam 27716175` and `./arroyo knoboff nvidia-kepler 27716175` (the OFF arm of `scanout_beam` is a constant `None`, every new item is at a file tail, the call site is a fold). Arm it with `UNAOS_BEAM=1` added to the flight line. **Unflown: nothing here is a metal fact until the witness below is read at the glass.** | F6 boot 1 (the defect); `./arroyo check` 0 both arches with `beam` newly named on the `x86-all` cfg leg — the ARMED `nvidia-kepler`+`beam` x86 configuration was type-checked by NOTHING before this; `knoboff beam` 0 and `knoboff nvidia-kepler` 0; q35 `test` 0 with the probe provably absent from the wire and provably present in the artifact. Witness to score, and the pre-flight scoring rules, in `docs/dev/OS/08_VIDEO/KEPLER-METAL-LOG.md` § PENDING METAL — BEAMX86 | the METAL verdict: `:: BEAMX86: … -> ARMED ::` at boot, then `[wc-h] … torn=` on the shell window under `storm` collapsing from 111 toward the vug windows' 3–10. An `ARMED` line with `torn=` UNCHANGED does not close this row — it converts it into "the counter that advances is not the raster the panel scans from" |

| A7 | GMUX switch to the iGPU does not persist (switches and restores on one call stack) | rmbp | rmbp | **flown** — 2026-09-16, flights 8 and 9: THE GATE IS FIXED ON METAL AND THE LADDER IS NOT, and the two boots disagree about the ladder.** Both flights: `:: igpu-dpy: pre-switch state DDC=0x02 SW_DISP=0x03 SW_EXT=0x01 SW_EXT_ST=0x21 DISP=0x03 EXT=0x21 sw_ext_state=UNACCEPTED ext_state=kepler-owned gate=ACCEPT ::` — `SW_EXT=0x01` no longer fails the gate, precisely GMUX-1's finding — then `:: igpu-dpy: restore ext=SKIPPED (write-target port, no state read) …`, so the external mux is left on IGD until power-cycle, deliberate and printed. The switch ran and `:: igpu: [GMUX] switch read-back mismatch (DDC is the only echo proven on this machine; DISP/EXT are advisory …): DDC=0x01 DISP=0x02 EXT=0x01 ::` on both. **⚠ THE LADDER VERDICT IS NOT REPRODUCIBLE ACROSS THE PAIR:** flight 8 `:: igpu-dpy: LADDER highest=05/10 name=end ok=1 pending=2 gmux=FAILED why=none elapsed_ms=10 ::`, flight 9 `:: igpu-dpy: LADDER highest=03/10 name=dpcd ok=0 pending=2 gmux=MATCH why=aux-timeout-error elapsed_ms=9 ::` — same machine, same register readings, one knob apart, and the boot that got FURTHER voted FAILED. `gate=ACCEPT` with the ladder at `highest=05/10` does not close this row, as the row itself says; the `gmux=`-vs-`highest=` disagreement is the next question and it needs no new code to ask. GMUX-2, folded (`exec-rmbp-gmux2`): the pre-switch gate reads the mux STATUS, not the write target, and the restore path never writes a value it did not read as state.** GMUX-1 convicted the gate: the one failing term of four was `SW_EXT=0x01` — the read of `GMUX_SWITCH_EXTERNAL` (0x40), the WRITE-TARGET port — scored against the STATUS encodings `{GMUX_EXTERNAL_DIS 0x03, GMUX_EXTERNAL_KEPLER_OWNED 0x21}`, while DDC=0x02, `READ_DISPLAY`=0x03 and `READ_EXTERNAL`=0x21 — every register that *reports* state — each read its accepted value. The file already knew: the POST-switch read-back was relaxed on exactly this reasoning ("write-side switch ports that do not echo their value would fail this comparison on a switch that WORKED"), and the PRE-switch gate was never reconciled with it. **The fix is one decision with two halves, because `p_ext` was also the RESTORE value** (`unwind.push_gmux(GMUX_SWITCH_EXTERNAL, p_ext as u8)`, at two sites) — dropping the gate term alone would have written `0x01`, a value no encoding in this file names, into the external display mux on every exit path including the self-test's immediate `unwind.execute()`: the silent state change the `GMUX_EXTERNAL_KEPLER_OWNED` doc-comment forbids. Half one: `gmux_preswitch_decode(ddc, read_display, read_external)`, a pure `const fn` at the file tail that **cannot** repeat the error because 0x40 is not one of its arguments, returning Accept / Refuse(port) / **Unreadable(port)** — 0x00, 0xFF and the 0xFFFFFFFF timeout sentinel are not mux states and no longer hide inside "not accepted". Half two: `ext_restore_target(read_external)` maps the ACCEPTED status read back to a write encoding only where the map is cited (`0x03` → `GMUX_EXTERNAL_DIS`, the same named constant on both sides) and otherwise returns `None` — on this machine (`0x21`, for which nothing in tree names a write encoding) the unwind pushes **no** EXTERNAL entry at all and the wire says `restore ext=SKIPPED (write-target port, no state read)`. Consequently EXTERNAL does not vote in the `gmux=` MATCH/FAILED verdict when it was not restored, and **G7's first metal run will leave the external mux on IGD until power-cycle** — deliberate, printed, and the price of not writing a guess. The `why` token stays `pre-switch-not-accepted` so flight 5's capture and the register's G6 row still compare; the discrimination rides the witness line as `gate=`. Knob-off byte-identity by construction: every new item carries the existing `all(target_arch = "x86_64", feature = "gmux_igd")` cfg and sits at the TAIL of `igpu.rs`, below the gmux harness — which is that file's last item — so no knob-OFF line number moves and `panic::Location` cannot shift (QUEUE B94). **Unflown: nothing here is a metal fact until the witness lines are read at the glass.** | `docs/dev/evidence/rmbp-0915/GMUX-1-PRESWITCH.md` (GMUX-1: flight 5 read back; 0x40 is the write-target port, the three status ports all read accepted); `./arroyo check` rc=0 both arches with the `gmux_igd` legs carrying the new `const _` pins — the decode is pinned by the BUILD, not by a `#[cfg(test)]` module, because `cargo test` never runs on this `no_std` crate (`kepler.rs:3906-3918`: "a test that cannot run is a comment that lies about being a test"); flight 5's own census `(0x02, 0x03, 0x21)` is one of those pins and asserts ACCEPT; `bash unaos/scripts/ledger-check.sh` rc=0 | the METAL verdict on a `UNAOS_GMUX_IGD=1` flight: `:: igpu-dpy: pre-switch state … SW_EXT=0x01 SW_EXT_ST=0x21 … gate=ACCEPT ::` followed by `:: igpu-dpy: rung=00 name=census ok=1 … ::`. `gate=ACCEPT` with the ladder still at `highest=00/10` does not close this row; `gate=UNREADABLE:*` re-opens G3, not this |

| A10 | **Quarry is not on the dock because it was never compiled into the flight image — Peter's *"there is no quarry"*, answered by one line the kernel printed at 38 s of boot.** `:: APPQUIT: app=quarry not compiled (UNAOS_QUARRY unset) — no window to quit :: SKIP ::` on BOTH flights, and `:: [midden] cmd="quarry" -> TerminalError len=44 ::` when it was typed at the shell. `UNAOS_QUARRY` is absent from both images' knob lines in the bench MANIFEST. **This is neither a board fact nor a reachability fact:** QUARRYX86 (the Finder lists `/` `/boot` `/apps` and the volumes on x86, 18 entries) and SMALLFIX3 (double-click launch) both landed this session, and the flight line simply did not arm them — so playbook item 9 pre-registered an observation the build could not produce, and an hour of Peter's bench time was spent looking for it. **The class is the one the track queue's own STATE line names three times (`arroyo` map + builder read + k8-reach registry) with a FOURTH place nobody gated: the flight line itself.** A knob whose feature the flight is meant to score, absent from the MANIFEST knob line, is invisible until the operator is at the glass. The repair is a pre-flight assertion that every feature the playbook's watch list names is on the knob line, run when the playbook is written rather than when the card is dry | rmbp | rmbp | open — found on flights 8 and 9, and *"there is no quarry"* was always TWO defects: the DOOR half is now **fixed-unflown**, the KNOB half is untouched and is what keeps this row open. Witness `[cursor11] … flicker_frames=<n> px_absorbed=<m>`, n counted at the PUBLICATION (bracket taken, arrow live, bytes actually written) and required 0; m is its control, because `flicker_frames=0 px_absorbed=0` only says the pointer never stood under a desktop present. Measured on the install path instead, same capture: the bracket is NOT deferring the arrow (`[wedge9] sprite-claim … refused=11475 masked=8462 retried=5 owed=1 serviced=2633` over the whole of flight 8 — `retried=5` counts the bounded UNMASKED retries that SUCCEEDED (`claim_bounded`, cursor.rs:403), so `repaint`'s own claim essentially never contended, and `owed=1 serviced=2633` is the handoff being CASHED rather than lost (`LOST` is the verdict reserved for refusals with nothing cashed and nothing pending). `refused` itself is a MIXED population and does not isolate the motion path — `owe_repaint` (cursor.rs:465) charges it from every call site, and 8462 of the 11475 are the composite side's masked asks — which is why the FOLD counter and not the claim counter is what decides this; `[cursor8] … requests=2658 repairs=2634 suppressed_rate=18` is 0.7 %), and it is NOT HID-side (`[deadman] hid=` 12–115 reports/s through the thirteen storm seconds the pad was talking, `pmp=` 316–964/s, `[schedx86] depth sent=` 1467 → 2231 across the storm). **The install collapses**: `GUI_FOLD_X86` (`[schedx86] … fold=`) is **0 for the whole boot up to 326403 ms** and **477** by 385196 ms against `sent` +764 — 62 % of the events the channel ACCEPTED during the storm were folded into another and installed nothing of their own (`fold` is Mouse-only while `sent` counts every class, so the motion-only fraction is higher, not lower); worst window 344857 → 349979 ms is `sent` +350 / `fold` +284 = ~66 installs for ~350 reports (**5.3 : 1, ~13 installs/s**, `[deadman] hid=` summing 332 over the same seconds) against a pre-storm control of `sent` +26 / `fold` +0 = **1 : 1** at 320755 → 326403 ms. Flight 9 is the same shape (`fold` 24 → 376 (24 already stood before that boot's storm, from the 57323 ms burst that left inflight=4), `sent` 242 → 884, `inflight=59` of 64 slots). **The cause is a BLITTER STALL, and it is upstream of the whole video-cursor arc**: every long `[deadman] gate=<core>/<age>` in either boot is `[wcser] PASS OVERDUE holder=c<N> age_ms=1000…4000 … at=span-flush … blit_inflight=1` with `blits_retired` FROZEN across all four one-second samples — four in flight 8 (c1 331–334 s, c2 371–374 s, c3 381–384 s, c4 390–393 s), three in flight 9 — and in each case that core IS the render core of the preceding `[schedx86] depth … (render core N)` line; the first ended `GATE STOLEN from c1 by c0 after 4030ms … REHOMED the render role from DEAD c1 to c2`. `x86_render_service` is the ONLY consumer of `GUI_CHANNEL_X86` and the ONLY caller of `pal::cursor::move_rel` on the x86 desktop (`main.rs:6845`, plus the routed twin through `user_input_route` → `track_routed` in the same drain), so while it spins in `span-flush` no pointer position installs at all, the channel fills, the producer coalesces (`main.rs:6172` — every second with `[deadman] in=26/297/1` or `in=41/631/3` has a gate hold in progress) and the drain's PTRCH fold (`main.rs:6761`) collapses the backlog into one event. One install, one jump. **THE REPAIR IS ONE OF TWO AND NEITHER IS IN `video/cursor.rs` OR `video/screen.rs`** (during the stall no pointer event has reached either file, so no bracket policy can move the arrow): (i) bound or break the `span-flush` blit wait so `COMP_GATE` is never held for seconds (`video/wm.rs`), or (ii) take the install off the render task and do it where the report is produced, in `x86_input_service` (`main.rs`), leaving routing and drag steering on the channel. The cheap repair this row asked for is built: the x86 UEFI loader takes three raw `rdtsc` stamps (its own entry, after `kernel.elf` is read, the last instruction before the jump), carries them in `BootInfo` as `tsc_loader_entry`/`tsc_loader_read`/`tsc_loader_jump` (`crates/boot-info`, ABI-LOCK offsets 256/264/272, `target_arch = "x86_64"` only — the aarch64 layout and the aarch64 loader path are byte-untouched), and the kernel converts them once, immediately after `apic::calibrate`, into ONE line ahead of any `BPACE` block: `:: BOOTCLOCK: firmware->loader=<ms> loader-read=<ms> loader-jump=<ms> kernel-entry=<tsc> tsc_hz=<n>(measured) ::`. Gate: `./arroyo check` rc=0 plus one `UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 ./arroyo test 120` rc=0 with the line present — QEMU's firmware phase is milliseconds, so that run proves the PATH and never the bench number. **THE ROW CLOSES ON THE NUMBER, NOT ON THE INSTRUMENT**: `firmware->loader` is an ABSOLUTE stamp counted from processor reset and still contains Peter's hand at the ⌥ picker, so it is a repeatable upper bound rather than a firmware timing, and the finding stands until a bench flight prints it and two flights of the same media are compared. Next step is the METAL row `BOOTCLOCK FLIGHT` in `docs/dev/OS/rmbp-queue.md`. | B4 | 2 cosmetic `unsafe` warnings at `bootloader/src/main.rs:1340/:1389` under `unaos_ivb` | rmbp | — | fixed-unflown — SMALLFIX-B4 (`exec-rmbp-smallfix`, parent `5e85a7a9`, 2026-09-15). **The row's line numbers were STALE; the sites were re-found by BUILDING the leg, not by reading them**: `crates/bootloader/src/main.rs:1389` and `:1438`, the OUTER `unsafe {` of `read_igpu_trace()` and `read_gmux_trace()`. Both are `unsafe fn`s in which every unsafe operation already sits in its OWN nested `unsafe {}` inside a closure (`outl`/`inl`/`read_mmio`; `outb`/`inb`/`wait_ready`), so the outer block guards nothing the nested blocks do not — which is what `unused_unsafe` says, and edition 2024's `unsafe_op_in_unsafe_fn` is why the nested blocks exist. **The fix is the KEYWORD, not an attribute**: `unsafe {` → `{`, two lines, line-neutral, no `#[allow]` — an allow would keep the lint quiet if a later edit put a real unsafe op directly in that block, while deleting the keyword keeps the compiler's guard. **An `unsafe` block emits nothing — PROVEN, not argued**: `UNAOS_IVB=1 ./arroyo esp-x86` before and after, then `cmp` of the two `EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI` → 12 bytes differ out of 74240 and all 12 are the PE BUILD STAMP, not code: the COFF `TimeDateStamp` at file offset 0x80 (the two low bytes of the Unix second the link ran — the builds were 5 minutes apart), the debug directory's copy of the same stamp at 0x11A9C, and the 8 CodeView build-id bytes at 0x11AB8 that lld derives by hashing the header. Every other byte of the loader is identical. One field cannot discriminate them, and discrimination is the patch's whole purpose. Verified and accepted: both `xhci/mod.rs` hunks N→N (`@2382 7→7`, `@4596 7→7`) so no `Location` moves from this file on any arch; the fourth ungated counter matches the convention this file documents for its three siblings at `:2427-2430` ("Bumped unconditionally … only the knob-gated witness prints"); no new board-named token, so S6 exposure does not grow. pi's re-accept is required (the aarch64 image moves) and is not covered by this grant. | B17 | **`./arroyo knoboff <feature>` — EXECUTOR-BRIEF §5 told every executor to run a command that did not exist.** The hedge ("when `./arroyo knoboff <feature>` exists, run it") had been in the head block since `aaaa62a0`, so the one step that MEASURES the knob-off byte-identity rule was never run: executors proved the append POSITION and reasoned about the bytes. It exists now in `unaos/arroyo` — it builds the knob-off loadable image at a baseline ref and at the caller's tree and compares them. Four decisions are the content, and each is a mistake that was available: (a) it compares the `llvm-objcopy -O binary` LOADABLE image, never `kernel.elf` (build-path + debug metadata + LLVM `.llvm.<hash>` .strtab suffixes — JB11 measured a pair differing by ONE loadable byte and 32 non-loaded ones) and never the ESP (it embeds SRC.TGZ, so it differs whenever the source does); (b) BOTH builds run in ONE throwaway worktree under the bench scratch root (path in the tool header), because a build's own path reaches its artifact (XUSBFW measured "in ONE directory" and `orindesk` "same worktree path" for exactly this reason) — a baseline built elsewhere puts a path delta and a code delta in the same number; (c) the working diff is snapshotted with `git diff HEAD` and applied into that tree, never `git stash` (one stack, every worktree); (d) every run also builds the tree with the feature ARMED and REFUSES a verdict unless that image differs on at least one arch — without it a misspelled feature, a feature whose sites are gated on a second feature nobody armed, and a knob this run never passed to cargo all report "byte-identical" in the same words a real pass uses. Exit status IS the deliverable (0 identical / 1 MOVED / 2 no verdict) and the dispatch arm propagates a non-zero return with an explicit early exit, so a verdict cannot fall through to the script's own 0 — B12's piped-gate lesson one layer down. **Scope stated in the tool and in its PASS line:** the x86_64 and aarch64-virt KERNEL images only, NOT `kernel8.img` (built from the curated `K8_FEATS`, which is SR1's class) and no staged media — a pi ack is still a pi ack. **The defect this seat made and caught in its own test run, worth more than the tool:** the first environment guard scanned for `UNAOS_*` in the env and refused, and fired on EVERY run, because BUILD-SHA-1 at `arroyo:54` exports `UNAOS_GIT_SHA` itself. A guard that trips on its own script is one everyone learns to override. It now asks the question it means — is the armed feature SET the default set — by re-entering `$0` with every `UNAOS_*` stripped and reading the `⚡ kernel features:` banner back, so the 1400-line knob map stays the single source. `UNAOS_GIT_SHA` is itself `option_env!`-embedded in the image, and all three builds inherit ONE arroyo's value: that is why knoboff drives cargo directly instead of calling the baseline tree's own `./arroyo`, whose sha would be its own | rmbp | host (no bench) | fixed-unflown — self-tested five ways, each quoted in the landing report: no-arg 2, undeclared feature 2 (names all 155 declared), armed env 2 (prints armed vs default set), PASS 0 end-to-end on `wc` at baseline `0bb37470` (x86 flat `img-x86:1923bcd2` 1,452,057 B; arm flat `img-arm:2ef8e1eb` 1,509,164 B; control fired both arches), and **GO-RED 1 by mutation** — one blank line at `allocator.rs:3` moved BOTH images, same size, exactly **1 byte** differing each: a single `panic::Location` line number incremented by one, which is the §5 failure mode caught at byte precision. Not yet run by an executor, not on trunk | `unaos/arroyo` `knoboff()`/`_knoboff_flat()`/`_knoboff_arm_csv()`; `docs/dev/EXECUTOR-BRIEF.md` §5 now unconditional. **Later the same day, pi 7's SP2 named the rule this tool had already been enforcing without saying so:** a before/after digest answers exactly ONE of two questions — guarding a baseline, or proving a change landed — and for a knob-gated change the knob-off artifact is identical BY CONSTRUCTION, so reading it as proof the wiring works is a null result dressed as a pass. knoboff's PASS is the first question and its ARMED CONTROL is precisely what stops it being mistaken for the second, since it refuses a verdict unless arming the feature moves something. The PASS line now says so out loud rather than leaving it implicit. **Then USING the tool found two defects in it that no reading had, which is now → P15.** `_knoboff_checkout` ran `git checkout --detach` before the `reset --hard` that clears the prior run's applied patch, so git aborted and the `&&` chain died: knoboff worked on a session's FIRST run and failed on every later one whose baseline differed from the throwaway tree's commit — it survived the original five proofs only because those baselines happened to equal it. And the new `baremetal` guidance was in the wrong arm: a Pi-only feature does not build-and-match, its ARMED BUILD FAILS on Pi-only imports (`piinstall` reaches for `crate::drivers::emmc2`), so the reader landed in the branch that told them to hunt a partner knob that does not exist. Both fixed; both proven by running the branch and reading its output, not by re-reading the diff; `docs/dev/evidence/rmbp13/LANDING-REPORT.md` (the five self-test runs, quoted) | — |

Green now, red later, on a row whose author followed the documented form. **The split shipped:** shared ids live in every tree (measured, 27 to 31 `S` rows on main, hw-jetson, hw-pi4 and hw-rmbp alike) so an unresolved `S<n>` stays RED; seat-prefixed ids are branch-local by construction (`SR` only on hw-rmbp, `SO` only on hw-jetson) so an unresolved one is DEFERRED — printed, counted and named in the summary line, never the silent skip that is P14's own worst verdict — and `UNAOS_LEDGER_STRICT=1` turns every deferral back into a red. **The landing runs strict**, which is the one tree where all three ledgers meet and a typo is distinguishable from a legitimate cross-branch reference. pi 7's preferred exit (skip refs inside the id cell) was declined with the reason: it exempts the id cell entirely, so a typo there is caught nowhere, ever. | B24 | **GATE-APPEND — LEDGER P7's trap, checked by machine instead of by eye.** P7's rule is that a statement folded onto an existing line for byte identity must sit BEFORE that line's first comment marker; after it, the statement IS comment text — it compiles nothing, `check` stays green, `strings` finds no witness, and the arc reports a pass for code that was never built. It bit ROOTFS's first build this round (a cfg'd `pub use` after a trailing marker), and every same-line-append grant this seat gave — BSPRUN, CRYSTAL A34, ROOTFS — was checked for it BY INSPECTION, per patch. That does not scale across three seats and nine executors, and it is the only condition in the grant checklist a machine can own outright. **The naive form is unshippable and this was measured, not argued:** flagging `#[cfg(` after a comment marker finds SEVEN lines in this tree and every one is prose — doc comments quoting cfg expressions (`video/strip.rs:62`, `video/dock.rs:46`, `arch/aarch64/smmu_tegra.rs:86`), some inside backtick spans that OPEN on an earlier line, which a per-line strip cannot see. A gate that reds on a sentence teaches people to disable it, which is knob-hygiene.sh's own lesson in this same tree. **The discriminator: the trap is a STATEMENT and an appended statement is the LAST thing on the line, so the comment text ends in a semicolon** — prose naming a cfg does not. That one condition takes the tree from seven false positives to zero while still firing on both real trap shapes. Control probe runs BEFORE the scan, in both directions, over built-in fixtures: three prose lines that must stay silent (one an UNBACKTICKED cfg mention, the hardest case) and two trap lines that must fire; either direction misbehaving exits 2, no verdict, because a scanner matching nothing would otherwise report a clean tree in the words a clean tree earns | rmbp | host (no bench) | fixed-unflown — three proofs, each run and reverted: a real trap injected into `video/pulsewin.rs` REDS at exit 1 naming file and line; **breaking the discriminator makes the CONTROL refuse a verdict at exit 2** rather than pass, naming the prose line it wrongly fired on, which is the proof the control guards the exact property; and the restored tree is green at exit 0 over 162 files. Wired into `check_both` beside GATE-KNOB, so it runs on every `./arroyo check`. **First real-world use, same day: pointed at orin's fold tip `1fba879f` after they hand-resolved a conflict by placing a statement on an existing line — OK over 164 files, controls firing both ways. That is this gate's highest-value moment**, because a conflict resolution is exactly where a statement gets nudged past a comment marker by hand and no compiler complains. It can be run against any branch's tip without a push: `git archive <sha> -- unaos/crates/kernel/src | tar -x -C <tmp>`, then point the script at it. **THEN IT MISSED A REAL DEFECT, AND THE MISS IS THIS SEAT'S TWICE OVER (KEYDOORS F0, orin 16 found it).** At `b768331a:main.rs:2948` — a fold THIS SEAT GRANTED — the A10 prose was written at column 139 and TABKEY's `#[cfg(feature = "tegra_el0")] if …wc_shell_focus_key(ev) { continue; }` sat at column 1734, inside it. A comment marker runs to end of line, so the Orin's TAB door was DEAD TEXT for an entire arc. **Every other signal said fine**, which is why care could not catch it: the line count never moved so the byte-identity proof was silent, `check` was green because the callee is a `pub fn` in a lib crate and loses no dead-code warning, and `git grep` still printed the line — which is HOW THE KEYDOORS AUDIT SCORED THAT DOOR WIRED. This seat's review passed it by verifying line-neutrality, which held; and this gate passed it because the discriminator hunts a statement APPENDED after a marker while this is a marker INSERTED ahead of live code. **A symmetric trap, and only one direction was ever asked about.** Second detector added: an unbackticked `#[cfg(` in the comment text with a statement terminator AFTER it — prose names a cfg, prose does not then open a block. Documented at `:148` and `:5365` (DEFAULT-QUIET). So their reading of `test_kernel8`'s first statement was correct and the arming simply happened earlier — not a defect, and their conclusion survives it. **SECOND sensitivity found in passing: `arroyo:54` exports `UNAOS_GIT_SHA` from `git rev-parse` and `arch/aarch64/genet.rs:2572` compiles it in via `option_env!`, so an image carrying that code depends on HEAD.** This seat first stated that UNSCOPED — *"a kernel8 image's bytes depend on HEAD"* — which was overreach of the same kind this round keeps recording. **orin 17 narrowed it and the narrowing is CORRECT, verified here:** `UNAOS_GIT_SHA` has exactly two references in the whole workspace, both in `genet.rs`; `arch/aarch64/mod.rs:186` gates that module `#[cfg(feature = "genet")]`; `genet` is a `K8_FEATS` arm (`UNAOS_GENET=1`), and no feature in the knob-off set reaches it — the Cargo closure of `baremetal`, `skip_xhci`, `witness`, `logts`, `bootlog` and `prtscrst` contains `genet` in none of them, and the only feature that implies `genet` at all is `nettest`. So the knob-off image is HEAD-INDEPENDENT, which is also what their two-HEAD measurement showed empirically. **RULE ADOPTED, two-part and SCOPED: a byte-identity claim names the RECIPE (`kernel8` vs `kernel8-test` differ by `witness`) always, and the HEAD whenever the image carries `genet`.** Resolves today's two knob-off values as one tree under two recipes. **Method note: the first measurement was VOID** — it sha'd `target/kernel8.img`, which does not exist (the image is `target/pi_baremetal/kernel8.img`), and three "No such file" results printed under a wrapper `exit=0`; three empty results read exactly like three matching ones. | B89 | **PETER'S FALSIFIER FOR "BOOT DUMB", AND THE TREE FAILS IT: the rMBP tells us at every boot that it has both disks he wants, and the OS binds to neither.** His question — *"i do not want to run catalina, i want unaos on the internal hd — then later boot an experimental version from the sd card slot"* — is not a feature request; he named it himself as a test of *"whether the OS is booting truly dumb or still containing hard codings"*. **It is the right falsifier and it fails.** The machine's own `[PCI-STOR]` census, on the wire in `capture/rmbp-gr8/ttyUSB1.log`, prints `bdf 0:31.2 8086:1e03 class=01 sub=06 progif=01 (sata) bar0=0x3080` — the Intel 7-series AHCI controller carrying the internal SSD Catalina lives on — and `bdf 3:0.1 14e4:16bc class=08 sub=05 progif=01 (sdhci) bar0=0xc1820000`, the internal SD slot. **Both targets are enumerated, printed, and driven by nothing.** **FOUR HARD-CODINGS, ranked by what they cost those two scenarios. (1) THE ATTACH STEP BINDS EXACTLY ONE CLASS: `drivers/pci.rs:53` is `if class_code == 0x0C && subclass == 0x03 && prog_if == 0x30` returning the first hit — one `if`, one class, xHCI. A dumb boot enumerates storage-class functions and attaches whatever driver fits; this one prints a census and attaches to none of it, which is the round's own family (printing is not binding) sitting in the boot path. (2) THE SET OF POSSIBLE DISKS IS A COMPILE-TIME ENUM: `fs/fat.rs`'s `BlockSource` is `Default`, `Usb`, `Sdhc` under `all(x86_64, sdhcblk)`, `TegraSd` under `tegra`+`sdmmc` — WHICH KINDS OF DISK CAN EXIST is decided at build time, per board, by cfg, and a dumb boot answers that at runtime. This is the general form of the BOOTROOT blocker raised at orin today (B87: `TegraSd` does not exist without `sdmmc`), and it is the deepest of the four. (3) EVEN THE DISK WE CAN DRIVE IS BEHIND A KNOB — `sdhcblk` gates the internal reader on x86, read-only. (4) `Default` MEANS "THE USB STICK" (`drivers/block.rs:182`, "the global slot IS the boot volume"), so the very idea of a default device is welded to one transport.** **COST OF EACH SCENARIO: the SD-slot experimental boot is CLOSEST — `drivers/sdhc.rs` already drives that controller and SDHC-4a made the first card write, so it needs 4b/4c plus hard-codings 1 and 3, and it needs ONE FREE ATTENDED BENCH FACT FIRST: does the firmware offer the internal SD slot as a startup disk under the option key at all. Nobody in the fleet knows, it costs no code, and every rung above it depends on the answer. The internal HD needs an AHCI driver that does not exist in the tree at all (`drivers/` is bcma, bench_ride, block, e1000, ehci, ehci_scout, emmc2, gpu, pci, sdhc, smc, xhci), plus 1 and 2 — but the census already hands rung 1 its BDF and BAR, so the first step is "read sector 0 of `0:31.2` and print it".** **THE HEADLINE FOR THE FLEET: the OS is boot-dumb about which VOLUME it uses — that is what BOOTROOT is fixing — and boot-SMART, meaning hard-coded, about which DEVICES CAN EXIST. Nobody was working on the second, and his question is what exposed it.** **⛔ REFRAMED BY PETER, AND THE RIGHT WORD CHANGES THE FINDING: *"dumb is not the right word but not making assumptions and not tying them together possibly staining the testing of the newer version."* The property is ISOLATION — two installs must not be tied together, or the older one contaminates the test of the newer. TWO STAINING VECTORS FOUND IN THIS LANE, both concrete.** **(1) THE BOOT-IDENTITY WINDOW IS NOT VERSION-UNIQUE.** BOOTROOT matches a named `.text` range of the running image against candidate files. **If the experimental build differs from the stable build OUTSIDE that window, the window is byte-identical in both, BOTH disks match, and first-found-wins can hand the experimental kernel the STABLE install's disk as root — the new version coming up on the old version's soil, which is the stain named.** The fix already ships in every image and is nearly dead: `UNAOS_GIT_SHA`, exported at `arroyo:52-54` as `git rev-parse --short=8 HEAD` and `option_env!`-embedded (`arroyo:6990`, "a byte the image carries"), whose only reader today is a Pi-only default-off HTTP body. **Put those bytes in the window and the match is version-unique by construction.** Sent to orin 22 as a condition with a two-build mutation proof. **(2) THE CAPTURE PATH ALREADY WRITES TO A FOREIGN DISK BY DESIGN.** `video/prtscr.rs:69-73`'s `mount_capture_target` is a documented fallback LADDER: rung 1 the program source when it admits writes, **rung 2 the dedicated USB mass-storage handle (`BlockSource::Usb`) when rung 1 is read-only or absent.** So an experimental boot whose own volume refuses writes will put `SCREEN<n>.PNG` on whatever other USB disk is present — in his scenario, the other install's. **The ladder was built for a good reason (flight-3's `BM_SUBSTITUTED` verdict left the program source unwritable on that bench) and it is a cross-install write with every gate green.** The rule it needs: a capture goes to the boot disk or REFUSES, never to a disk this boot did not come from — auto-selecting a foreign volume is the stain, not the read-only mount. **⭐ AND R27's REASON NAMES THE DESTINATION THIS ROW IS BLOCKING, which nothing else in the round connected: Peter, 2026-09-09 — "THIS WILL HELP WHEN WE START SELF HOSTING WHERE WE WILL BE REBOOTING THE VERY MACHINE WE JUST WROTE THE CODE ON … THE MACHINE ITSELF IS COMPILING AND TESTING FIRST." SELF-HOSTING ON THE rMBP IS THE INTERNAL-HD GOAL, and this row's four hard-codings are its prerequisites: a machine cannot compile, test and reboot itself on a disk its own kernel cannot enumerate. The census prints the AHCI controller every boot and nothing binds it.** Two other rows become prerequisites of the same destination rather than independent findings: **B91** (the installer guards HOME and not STRANGERS, so the AHCI driver must not land before the stranger guard — and a self-hosting machine writes to its own disk as NORMAL OPERATION, which is precisely the traffic that guard has to distinguish from touching Catalina) and **B90** (friend-invariance — a self-hosting box with an experimental card in the reader is two installs on one machine by construction, which is the state that rule exists for). **The order is therefore fixed by the goal, not by preference: stranger guard, then AHCI, then self-host.** And the reason carries its own limit, which this lane should not overread — "the code is still generalized": the focus is on whose VERDICT comes first, never a licence to narrow what the code presumes about a machine. | rmbp | rmbp bench | flown — 2026-09-16, flights 8 and 9: **AHCIFLY PASSED ON PETER'S OWN SSD, and the row's headline sentence is no longer true of the SATA controller.** `:: AHCI: port=0 model="APPLE SSD SM768E" sectors=1467339812 lba48=1 ::`, `:: AHCI: port=0 sector0 sig=0xaa55 kind=GPT ::`, `:: AHCI: registered port=0 as registry index 0 — blocks=1467339812 (716474 MiB) READ-ONLY (global BLOCK_DEVICE untouched, installer not told) ::`, `:: AHCI: selfcheck port=0 identify=ok sector0=GPT -> PASS ::`, then `[bootdisk] volume source=ahci port=0 vol=EFI serial=0x67e317ed blocks=1467339812 volumes_by_content=2 ::` and `[bootdisk] ahci census: sata_sources=1 with_fat_volume=1 carrying_AHCIBOOT.TXT=0 ::` — byte-identical on flight 9. | B90 | **FRIEND-INVARIANCE — Peter's principle turned into a falsifiable boot test, and it catches two defects this lane already found.** His words, 2026-09-08: *"seeing another UnaOS disk is like seeing a friend — nobody wants another to wrap their life around them just because they're acquainted."* It refines his earlier *"home soil and nothing more"* ruling by naming what recognition must NOT become: **acknowledging kin is fine; reorganising yourself around it is not.** **THE INVARIANT, stated so a machine can check it: an install's observable behaviour is IDENTICAL whether or not another UnaOS disk is present, except for the witness lines that name the friend. Any other difference is entanglement and is a defect.** **THE FALSIFIER, and it is cheap on a bench because it is one unplug: boot the same image twice, once with the friend disk present and once without, and diff the wire modulo the mount witnesses. A clean diff is the invariant holding; every surviving line is an entanglement with a name and a line number.** **IT WOULD HAVE CAUGHT BOTH OF THIS ROUND'S STAINING VECTORS WITHOUT EITHER BEING SUSPECTED: (1) `video/prtscr.rs`'s `mount_capture_target` ladder sends `SCREEN<n>.PNG` to rung 2, the USB mass-storage handle, when the boot volume refuses writes — so captures LAND SOMEWHERE ELSE depending on whether a friend is plugged in, which is a wire-visible difference in exactly the diff above. (2) A boot-identity window that is not version-unique lets root itself change with the friend present, which is the largest possible difference and the one Peter named as staining the newer version's test.** **THE THIRD CASE THE TEST FINDS AND NOBODY HAS LOOKED FOR: anything that becomes load-bearing only when the friend is there. A path under an indexed bus mount point exists only while that disk is attached, so any behaviour that references it is conditional on a friend being present — an install that behaves differently alone than in company has wrapped its life around an acquaintance, whether or not it ever writes a byte.** Not built this round (support round, zero executors); queued as an x86 bench leg and offered to orin for render11, where the Orin has the same shape with its slot card and reader card.  **⇒ FLOWN 2026-09-09 ON THE ORIN (orin 23, render11 friend-diff; three-boot form). THE RESULT IS THE PAIR OF NUMBERS, NOT THE SURVIVOR LIST: same-condition A1 vs A2 = 101 survivors, cross-condition A1 vs B = 102, SAME FAMILIES AT THE SAME COUNTS. So the friend contributes about ONE family and the baseline noise is measured rather than assumed — the falsifier this seat proposed (diff the two friend-ABSENT boots, which orin already held) is what separated signal from noise, and it retired the earlier "64 survivors, all classified".** Normalization was PRE-REGISTERED by this seat before the capture existed — counters, timestamps, addresses and the mount witnesses only; no line drops, order kept — because the failure mode of this test is tuning the filter until the diff is clean, at which point it can no longer fail. orin re-ran under that set and published both passes side by side, declaring what the first pass had done that the strict set forbids (dropped whole census families rather than their counters; three allowed-list entries added AFTER seeing the cross-condition diff). **THE CAUSE OF THE LOAD-ADDRESS DELTA IS NOW NAMED AND IT IS FIRMWARE: `HEAP-GUARD` reports 153 carveout ranges friend-absent and 165 friend-present — the UEFI map carries TWELVE MORE with the card in, which is why the kernel landed 0x3f0000 lower. Not UEFI whimsy.** ACCEPTED as benign, with the criterion written down so "consumed honestly" cannot absorb the next survivor: **a survivor is benign IFF UnaOS's behaviour is a PURE FUNCTION of the changed input AND the change is not UnaOS-caused.** This one passes traceably. ⚠ **BUT IT IS A LIVE COUPLING AND NOT A CLOSED ITEM: nothing UnaOS does today makes a different DECISION because of the load address — the x86 specs wildcard `img=[…]` (`x86-witness.spec:547-548` records two rMBP boots differing only in `ehdr`/`img=`, unconnected until tonight) and B106's STAMP-MATCH keys on the build sha. The moment anything keys on an absolute address or a load-derived span, the friend gains a channel to change behaviour THROUGH FIRMWARE without touching a line of our code.** Named channel, currently harmless. **⛔ AND VECTOR 1 WAS NOT TESTED — it declined to trigger, which is not the same as passing, and this is where the owed positive control gets its DESIGN. `mount_capture_target` falls to the USB rung only WHEN THE BOOT VOLUME REFUSES WRITES. The flight's ten `:: PRTSCR:` lines all went `-> OK` to the ROOT volume, so the ladder never entered its firing condition; ten green captures say nothing about the rung.** So the §6 control is not "fly it again with the friend" — it is **FRIEND PRESENT *AND* ROOT REFUSING WRITES**, the only configuration in which vector 1 can stain and the one this row was written to catch. Either outcome beats another clean pair: captures landing on the friend falsifies the invariant, a refusal that says so closes the vector. **Verdict as orin framed it and this seat endorses: not falsified on one image and one pair; the positive control is owed. B90's falsifier ran; ITS OWN falsifier has not.** | rmbp | rmbp + orin benches | open — invariant stated, the falsifier flown once on the Orin, and B90's OWN falsifier still owed: the §6 positive control is FRIEND PRESENT **and** ROOT REFUSING WRITES, the only configuration in which vector 1 can stain. B109's witness field, the prerequisite, is now landed, so the control is buildable. JOB: `docs/dev/OS/rmbp-queue.md` METAL (one unplug and a diff). Sweep 2026-09-15 | `docs/dev/OS/rmbp-ledger.md` B89 for the two staining vectors it catches, `unaos/crates/kernel/src/video/prtscr.rs` at `mount_capture_target` for the capture ladder, and `docs/dev/evidence/rmbp16/FOCUS-QUEUE.md` for the queued leg | → B89 |

Rung B, a first write onto a blank or foreign card, **HAS NO POSITIVE EVIDENCE and the product must not manufacture one** — what it can do is make the operator's act specific instead of general: show what is actually on the disk and require the operator to type back something readable only FROM that disk (its volume label, its size and model as we read them). | B114 | **THE CAPTURE RING SILENTLY ATE THE HEAD OF EVERY BOOT, AND THE ONE LINE WRITTEN TO ANNOUNCE THAT WAS ITSELF UNOBSERVABLE — a diagnostic that can only be read by someone already watching.** Found while fixing B112. The 2012 rMBP has no 16550 (`SERWIT-1: contended serial [uart16550=absent carrier=ftdi-mirror …]`), so its ONLY carrier is the boot-capture ring in `drivers/xhci/ftdi.rs` — drop-oldest, replayed at `ftdi:console-up`. **Both 2026-09-16 boots ran it at capacity:** replay spans of **260 958 bytes (flight 8)** and **258 584 bytes (flight 9)** against `CAP` = **262 144** — a margin of 1 186 and 3 560 bytes, i.e. 1.004x and 1.014x, on a buffer whose own doc comment claimed "4x headroom". Each replay begins MID-TOKEN (flight 9's first bytes are `=2 n=15`), which is the drop-oldest signature. **WHAT IT COST, measured by counting literals over the whole 11 265-line capture:** `:: x86 bar1exp: UC arm ARMED` 0 hits (the entire point of flight 9), `:: x86 fb-wc: retyped` 0 hits (the BASELINE arm, on the build that certainly emitted it — the control proving this is transport and not a cfg arm), and `:: video: WRITER seeded` 0 hits (UNCONDITIONAL on every x86_64 build). B112 was opened, flown and investigated against an absence that was never evidence about the kernel at all. **AND THE INSTRUMENT FOR EXACTLY THIS EXISTED AND WAS ALSO LOST:** CAPWIT's `emit_verdict` writes `:: FTDI-CAP: … TRUNCATED — N byte(s) LOST off the head …` — correctly, straight into the DMA staging buffer AHEAD of the replay so it cannot evict what it reports on. That makes it the FIRST thing on the cable, emitted before a bench operator's `cat /dev/ttyUSB0` has the port open: **`FTDI-CAP` = 0 hits across both boots while the ring was pinned at capacity.** The announcement was lost to a different mechanism than the loss it announced, which is why the truncation stayed silent through three flights. **THE CLASS, and it is not about FTDI:** a witness emitted before its own carrier exists is unfalsifiable, and a verdict said only once, only on the earliest channel, is not a verdict. Fix (PHASE31WIT, same commit): `late_verdict()` re-says it as an ordinary console line well inside the live stream — `:: FTDI-CAP: replayed=<bytes> cap=<bytes> lost=<bytes> head_cut=<y-or-n> ::`, latched to one line, printed from `bootpace::service_dump`; `REPLAYED` counts what the cable actually received so saturation is a field and not an arithmetic exercise on the log file afterwards; and `CAP` rises 256 KiB → 1 MiB (`.bss`, NOBITS, zero image growth), restoring a real ~4.0x margin. Both channels now carry it, with different failure modes. | rmbp | rmbp | fixed-unflown — found 2026-09-16 while measuring B112; the fix needs no metal, but only a bench boot scores `head_cut=n` at the new `CAP`. QEMU covers the mechanism (`UNAOS_USBSERIAL=1` FTDI carrier: late line present, `lost=0`), with the go-red shown by shrinking `CAP` to 4 KiB → `lost>0 head_cut=y`; FBWCWIT (same class, same commit day): the default arm's witness now re-emits too — `bar1exp_witness()` renamed `fb_wc_witness()`, one caller, the UC arm's own line unchanged | the 2026-09-16 flight-8/flight-9 capture (`capture/rmbp12-flight8/ttyUSB0.log`): replay spans measured between the first replayed line and `BOOTLOG: [ … ] ftdi:console-up`, literal counts via `LC_ALL=C grep -a -c -F`; `unaos/crates/kernel/src/drivers/xhci/ftdi.rs` (`CAP`, `emit_verdict`, `late_verdict`) | → B112 |
| B115 | **x86 HAD NO KERNEL-STACK INSTRUMENT OF ANY KIND, AND THE RENDER SERVICE WAS ALREADY AT 95 % OF THE BLANKET 16 KiB.** `arch/x86_64/sched.rs` had no guard, no canary, no paint and no high-water probe — `spawn_inner` allocated `alloc::vec![0u8; TASK_STACK_SIZE]` and an overflow walked out of the bottom of that heap block into whatever the allocator had placed below it, silently. aarch64 has had the paint, the `[u7stk]` probe and the `STACK_REDZONE`/`STACK_HIGHGUARD` absorber since U7STK/SHELLUP/REDZONE; x86 had none of it, so the x86 half of the Pi's dsktp-boot-11 class was invisible by construction. **QUARRYX86-2 (`d889369a`, its STOP 2) is what made it owed:** that arc's click-router arm calls `quarry::service()` -> `open()` — panel read + two VFS `read_dir`s + a surface alloc — and on this arch the only routers that reach it are `kernel_main` (the BOOT stack, not a `Task`) and `x86_render_service`, spawned with the blanket 16 KiB. **MEASURED FIRST, and the measurement is the finding:** the instrument was built, armed at the UNCHANGED 16 KiB, and run — `UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_QUARRY=1 UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 ./arroyo test 120`, rc=0, MBENCH 6/6, spec replay green, 16 `:: STACK:` lines over a 145.5 s capture. `render` climbs 6552 -> 6704 and then PLATEAUS at **`high=15600 of 16384` for the last 13 dumps: 784 bytes of headroom, 95.2 % consumed** — and that is the ORDINARY pass, because this leg arms no pointer typist (`UNAOS_XHCIHUB`), delivers no `[dock] press`, and therefore **does not contain the QUARRYX86-2 chain at all**. 16 KiB was not about to become too small; it was already spent. **THE FIX:** `spawn_stack` (the x86 twin of aarch64's, `spawn` plus a caller-sized usable span) and `RENDER_PATH_STACK_SIZE` = 32 KiB at both render spawn sites (`main.rs`, LINE-NEUTRAL — 9905 lines before and after, both inside `cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")` regions, so no aarch64 byte moves and the call is RENAMED in place rather than a line inserted). Arithmetic, not precedent: the frames below the click router enumerated on the Pi's armed ELF for the identical `quarry::open()` body are `wc_click_route` 288 + `quarry::live::open` 928 + the deeper subtree (repaint 992 + text 1232 + `wm::create_at` 64 + `wm::composite` 6736 = 9024, against the listing side's 4224) = **10240 B and that is a floor**; `32768 - 15600 = 17168 B` left above the measured pass (1.7x the chain, and 32 KiB is 2.1x the pass), where `16384 - 15600 = 784 B` puts the first dock press ~9.2 KiB past its floor. **CORROBORATED at the new size**, which is the check that 15600 was a depth and not an artefact of the stack it was read on: the same command at 32 KiB reports `:: STACK: render high=15536 of 32768 ::` (20 dumps, 120.5 s wall, rc=0, MBENCH 6/6) — two independent runs on two DIFFERENT sizes agreeing to **64 bytes**, which a saturating gauge cannot do, so `headroom=17232` (52.6 %) is a sampled margin and not an assumed one. It also agrees with the Pi's `RENDER_STACK_SIZE` / `PUMP_PATH_STACK_SIZE` / `U7_LAUNCH_STACK_SIZE`, which is a CHECK on the number and not its source. `TASK_STACK_SIZE` is UNTOUCHED — raising the blanket charges every kernel task for one path. **THE GUARD:** `STACK_GUARD` = one 4 KiB page of `GUARD_FILL` below every x86 kernel stack (all three allocation sites), read by `guard_state` at its two ends and checked in `run()` right after `switch_context` returns — the aarch64 switch-OUT site, because the low guard is single-writer and that sample is both first and the only one that can see a TRANSIENT dip. **It is a poisoned span and not an unmapped page, and that is measured, not preferred:** `linked_list_allocator` writes its free-list `Hole` node into the START of a freed block (the guard would fault `dealloc` with the heap lock held); the heap is a window in the FIRMWARE's identity map that also backs xHCI/e1000 DMA at physical==bus, so a 4 KiB hole needs a live 2 MiB leaf split; and there is no kernel-space TLB shootdown in this tree (core-local `invlpg` + `AS_GEN` cover USER leaves only). Same question, same answer and the same three-part shape as aarch64's REDZONE arc. Action is GRADUATED per `scheduler.md` §2.y: `entered` reports once (rate-limited, 16-line cap + `GUARD_LAST` id, because a breached guard is persistent state that nothing re-paints) and the task CONTINUES; `TRAVERSED` PANICS NAMED, which is the outcome the arc exists for — the alternative is a fault on a stack the handler cannot use, i.e. a triple fault and a silent reset with nothing on the wire. **WHAT THE GUARD CAUGHT ON ITS FIRST ARMED RUN — a SECOND mis-sized x86 task, and it is this row's STOP:** `:: STACK: task=u7x-launch overflow guard hit sp=0x333df60 cpu=3 id=19 low=0x333c000 size=16384 guard=4096 entered ::`, with `ctx_rsp` back IN range — the transient dip no park-time bounds test can see, the x86 twin of the `shell-run` finding aarch64 made, and the twin of the Pi's `u7-launch` which was sized to 32 KiB while its x86 counterpart stayed on the blanket. Repair is one call at `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs:23795` (`spawn` -> `spawn_stack(…, 32 * 1024)`); OUT of this arc's named files, reported and NOT taken. It REPRODUCES on the 32 KiB gate run at a different address (`sp=0x3342210` — the render allocation moved), so it is a property of that task and not a one-off. Guard cost is unconditional (two volatile byte reads per switch, 4 KiB heap per task) because a protection present only on witness builds does not protect the build that dies at the bench; the paint and the `:: STACK: <task> high=<bytes> of <size> ::` probe are `witness`-gated and ride `emit_load_witness`'s existing ~5 s gate, so `esp-x86` media carries neither symbol and no clock was added | rmbp | rmbp | fixed-unflown — 2026-09-16, branch `exec-rmbp-rendstack`, parent `11ca67f1`. Gates: `./arroyo check` rc=0 both arches; `UNAOS_WC=1 UNAOS_QUARRY=1 UNAOS_QEMU_FULL=1 ./arroyo test 120` rc=0 with `:: STACK: render high=` present, go-red by shrinking the size to 4 KiB and reverting — see the commit body. Byte identity: every x86 image moves (the guard is ungated and `sched.rs` changes); aarch64 is untouched BY CONSTRUCTION — `arch/x86_64` is not declared on that arch (`arch/mod.rs` `cfg_if`, so the file is never lexed) and both `main.rs` edits are line-neutral inside `cfg(target_arch = "x86_64")` regions. **Needs no metal to score the mechanism; a bench boot is what scores the number under a real dock press.** U7XSTACK 2026-09-16 (branch `exec-rmbp-u7xstack`, parent `66b89c75` = the RENDSTACK fold): the second task is sized — `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs:23795` `spawn` -> `spawn_stack(…, 32 * 1024)`, folded onto the one line, 24179 lines before and after, ungated by design (a size, not an instrument; no `knoboff`). `./arroyo check` rc=0 both arches; the same test command rc=0 (mode=full, completion at serial line 2295, MBENCH 6/6, 130.2 s wall): `awk 'index($0,":: STACK: task=u7x-launch") && index($0,"entered ::")' target/serial.log` counts 0 lines against 1 on each RENDSTACK capture, with `:: U7x: … -> PASS ::` present as the control that the launcher ran; `:: STACK: render high=15536 of 32768 ::` x22. The launcher has no `high=` line of its own (the probe reads only the task driving the service dump), so the guard's silence is the measurement. Go-red at 8 KiB: `entered` then `TRAVERSED`, the named panic at `sched.rs:7530`, rc=1, completion=truncated; reverted, check rc=0 again | the 16 KiB measurement capture (16 `:: STACK:` lines, `render high=15600 of 16384` x13, the `u7x-launch` guard hit); `unaos/crates/kernel/src/arch/x86_64/sched.rs` (`STACK_GUARD`, `GUARD_FILL`, `STACK_POISON`, `RENDER_PATH_STACK_SIZE`, `spawn_stack`, `paint_stack`, `guard_state`, `stack_guard_check`, `stack_high_water`, `emit_stack_witness`); `docs/dev/OS/02_KERNEL_CORE/scheduler.md` §2.aa | → QUARRYX86-2 `d889369a` (its STOP 2) |
| B116 | **SPANFLUSH STOPPED: THE SPAN-FLUSH "BLIT WAIT" THAT CHOP NAMED AS REPAIR (i) DOES NOT EXIST, so it cannot be bounded in `video/wm.rs` — and the capture contradicts A1's own status cell.** The phase-33 site (`comp_mark(r.id, 33)`, wm.rs:21219) is a per-row loop of `blit_traced` → `FrameBuffer::blit` → one `copy_nonoverlapping` into BAR1 (framebuffer.rs:481): no fence, no semaphore, no PFIFO get pointer, no polling loop (population: every statement of `blit_traced`, `FrameBuffer::blit` and the row loop wm.rs:21219 to the band's `beam::settle`). `blit_inflight=1` means the holder entered that copy and never came out; a budget cannot run on a core that is not executing, which WEDGEINJ's header (wm.rs:9700) already states and injects with `cli`+spin. Whole-capture literal counts (`LC_ALL=C grep -a -c -F`, control `SPAN-FLUSH TIMEOUT`=0): `PASS OVERDUE` 28, `GATE STOLEN` 7, `REHOMED the render role` 7, `is DEAD and its singleton` 7, `revenants=1` 0 — every hold ended in the steal at 4016-4303 ms and no stolen-from core ever returned. Six of seven are `at=span-flush blit_inflight=1` with `blits_retired` frozen across four 1 Hz samples; the seventh (flight 8 c4, 390982 ms) is `at=pw-exit blit_inflight=0` — CHOP's "7 of 7" is 6 of 7. Flight 9 (`aperture=uc`) stalled identically; the root port read `d_lnksta=0000 d_devsta=0000 aer=n` at all 28 samples. Healthy passes on the same boot: `[comp2] pass_us=6724 max_us=13966` (322146 ms), `pass_us=18239 max_us=35297` (327577 ms); the record honest pass is 302 ms; `COMP_PASS_OVERDUE_MS`=1000 and `COMP_GATE_STEAL_MS`=4000 (wm.rs:9486, 9496) are already the only bound another core can enforce. The brief's `SPAN-FLUSH TIMEOUT … -> RELEASED` line would be `GATE STOLEN` under a second name; the cost per event (one dead core, one lost window — flight 8 paid it four times in 63 s and ran render on c5) is not changed by shortening the freeze. Expressible in wm.rs but a DECISION, not taken: `COMP_GATE_STEAL_MS` 4000→1500 (5x the record), or a signature-specific earlier steal (phase 33 + inflight=1 + odometer frozen two samples). The repair is W5 (`PCIE-RP-RECOVERY.md` §11.3), in the Kepler driver, outside this brief. AND: A1's status cell says there was no `REHOMED` and no `DEAD c<n>` on either boot — the capture has seven of each (four flight 8, three flight 9). | rmbp | rmbp | open — STOPPED 2026-09-16 (branch exec-rmbp-spanflush, docs only, no kernel byte moves); the seat amends A1's cell and rules on the steal-threshold option; the fix is A1's W5 rung — **the steal-threshold option is TAKEN: fixed-unflown for the freeze LENGTH only (STEALMS 2026-09-16, branch `exec-rmbp-stealms`, parent `5ebe29f4`; tip sha at the fold — a commit cannot name its own hash): `COMP_GATE_STEAL_MS` 4_000 -> 1_500 (`video/wm.rs:9496`, line-neutral; `comp_steal_pick`'s `after_render` becomes 1_500 with the render core as corpse, 1_750 otherwise, rung 2 at +250). The freeze becomes ~1.5 s, the next stall prints `GATE STOLEN … after 15xx ms`, the core is STILL LOST and the per-boot steal count is unchanged; W5 stays the repair. A1's cell is corrected in the same commit.** | the 2026-09-16 flight-8/flight-9 capture (`capture/rmbp12-flight8/ttyUSB0.log`, boots split at line 9027): stall lines via `awk` on `PASS OVERDUE` and `GATE STOLEN`, literal counts via `LC_ALL=C grep -a -c -F`; `unaos/crates/kernel/src/video/wm.rs` (`blit_traced`, `comp_gate_release`, `wcser_overdue_probe`, `COMP_GATE_STEAL_MS`), `unaos/crates/kernel/src/video/framebuffer.rs` (`blit`); `docs/dev/OS/08_VIDEO/engine.md` §SPANFLUSH | → A1 |

| B119 | **W5 IS SCOPED, AND THE CAPTURE ALREADY FAILS ITS LINK-SIDE BRANCHES: after every one of the seven steals of flights 8 and 9 the next holder's CPU blits into the SAME BAR1 window completed within 70–369 ms (f8) or one 5 s rollup (f9), while the parked core's one store never retired — `revenants=0` on all 96 `[wcser]` rollups over windows up to 60.7 s.** Posted credits are per link and the root port's posted egress is a FIFO (PCIe r3.0 §2.4.1 Table 2-40: no posted request passes an earlier one), so a store queued at the port under W5(a) credit exhaustion or W5(b) a stalled GPU window would have left ahead of the next holder's blits and the core would have been a revenant; seven times, none. Both standing and transient forms fail; what stands is (d) a per-core transaction lost or unacknowledged UPSTREAM of the root port, invisible to every PCIe register — M1's shape with the WC premise W4 removed. Every stall tabled with its `awk`: t0, holder, stealer, `at=`/phase, win/row/`blit_aim`/`blit_inflight`, `blits_retired` frozen on 4 samples and its delta to the next stall, the 28 flat `wedge-sample` lines, the last `[comp2]`/`[wcser]`/`[deadman]` before each hold, and the driver's last line per family: 0 Kepler-driver lines within 60 s of either storm (`awk` over `:: kepler:`, `KFBIND:`, `KDHEAD:`, `kdisp:`, `[NVIDIA]`, `PFIFO` with `ts >= STORM_begin-60000`, both boots), `grep -c 'kepler_ce::' video/wm.rs` = 0 and `grep -c 'kepler_fifo::' video/wm.rs` = 0, gmux `ext_state=kepler-owned` on both boots — every stall is a pure CPU blit with the GPU idle from the driver's side. The BAR1 the CPU is parked in is the 256 MiB VRAM aperture at 0x90000000, GOP surface at +0x20000, mapping inherited from Apple's firmware and never programmed or read by this kernel (identity at the one probed page, `bar1-identity VERDICT IDENTITY`). **THE INSTRUMENT DOES NOT FIT `pcihealth.rs` AND IS A STOP:** a BAR0 or ECAM read of the endpoint from the sampler path is a non-posted request that cannot pass the stuck posted write (the same ordering rule), so under the very branch W5 names it is never transmitted, no completion timeout runs, and the service core — this laptop's only serial carrier — parks; the safe RP-side additions (primary PCI Status `0x06`, the ext-cap ID chain) carry no posted-write fact and would print flat, and `blits_retired_total` is private to `wm.rs`. The two instruments that would answer are named with their files and outcome tables in PCIE-RP-RECOVERY §12.3: I1 a post-steal BAR0 dump from the stealer at `wm.rs:4888` (`:: W5: site=post-steal …`, reads PMC_INTR_0/PFIFO_INTR/`0x70000`/`0x1804` plus the UNPINNED nouveau set PBUS_INTR_0 `0x1100`, PRI fault `0x9084`/`0x9088`, fault mask `0x259c`, BAR1 fault record `0x2840..0x284c`) and I2 an NMI probe of the dead core (`:: W5: nmi core=c<n> rip=<x> …`; a hardware-parked core never takes it, a software spin does — the one cheap test nothing has ever run). Repairs W5 would license, costed and NOT built: SBR + re-takeover (dark panel, frees nothing under (d)); no CPU store ever targets BAR1 (render to host memory, CE copies — the whole dark Kepler channel ladder, KF27 STILL-DARK); | B117 | **THE INSTALL CANNOT MOVE TO THE PRODUCER INSIDE `main.rs` — the render path installs a relative report in TWO places and only one is in that file; what is built is the ledger the chop can be read from directly.** CHOP measured flight 8's chop as a 5.3 : 1 install collapse under storm (`[schedx86] depth … fold=` +284 vs `sent` +350) and named the render core's span-flush stall as the cause, with the repair "take the install off the render task and do it where the report is produced". MEASURED FIRST (read-only in `pal.rs`, `video/cursor.rs`, `video/wm.rs`): `pal::cursor::move_rel` IS safe to call from `x86_input_service` whole — `POS` is a masked constant-time spinlock, `SPRITE` is `claim_bounded(2 ms)` → `owe_repaint`, `WRITER` is a single-statement copy, `TABLE` may not block by rule, `stage_window` holds no `SpriteLoan` across mark 33 (`wm.rs:21193-21219`), and one-core-installs-while-another-composites already exists via the ring-3 present chains. No `cursor.rs` split is needed. THE STOP IS THE SECOND INSTALL SITE: `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs:5701-5708` `user_input_route` → `pal::cursor::track_routed` installs the same delta on the consumed branch whenever a ring-3 app holds focus (the bench case), so a producer-side install with the real delta still on the channel DOUBLES the motion; the delta cannot be zeroed (`pack_input` → `INPUT_EV_MOUSE_REL`, `user-vug/src/main.rs:2056` drags by it) and cannot be compensated from `main.rs`. EXACT CHANGE, NOT MADE (four sites, two outside the brief): (1) `syscall.rs:5701-5708` delete the `track_routed(&ev)` call, rewrite the CURSOR-VUG paragraph; (2) `pal.rs` retire `track_routed`; (3) `main.rs:6845` the `Mouse` arm keeps `draw_over`, drops `move_rel`; (4) `main.rs:6165` the producer calls `move_rel` before the offer/fold and charges `PTRI_INSTALLS`. `MouseAbsolute` untouched.
