## D. Bench facts this track measured (not defects — no status; the command that measured each is the evidence)

- **`line-butler.py` has NO x86 stream**, and `NVIDIA` in its ORIN_MARKS mis-routed 81% of an rMBP boot into `orin.log` (fixed by orin's BUTLERMARK). Never point the butler at the rMBP's FTDI. Measured: `awk '/NVIDIA/' capture/rmbp9-flight5/ttyUSB0.log` = 12 hits, first at line 1601 of 8651. (`docs/dev/evidence/rmbp10/BUTLER-NVIDIA-MISROUTE.md`)
- **The FTDI re-enumerates** (`ttyUSB0`→`ttyUSB1` on 2026-09-03). Identify by `ls /dev/serial/by-id/usb-FTDI_*`; kill bridges by `lsof -t /dev/ttyUSBn`, never `pkill -f`. (F6)
- **`stage-x86.sh` is two-pass by design**: pass 1 creates the tree and refuses (exit 5); name the tree in the playbook; re-run with the same `--stamp`. (F6, F7 staging)
- **Score what BOOTED by the mapped span**: `WXN-x86 … img=[lo,hi)` vs `readelf -lW kernel.elf` LOAD span — F5 0x1351290 · F6 0x134f198 · F7 0x134fca8. The wire prints no sha. (F6)
- **The reboot ladder's post-verb tail is lost on the rMBP by construction** (A3); never score its absence as a failure. (F6)
- **`wc_shim` is an INLINE mod** at `arch/x86_64/syscall.rs:4777` — a filename search finds nothing. (B11)
- **The `⚡ kernel features:` banner is a BUILD-LOG line and can never appear on a serial wire — verified: the only emitters are shell `echo`s in `unaos/arroyo` (`:1613`, `:4790`, `:6148`), and `grep` over the whole kernel source finds NO emitter, only a `compile_error!` string and one doc comment naming it.** Raised by orin 20 (2026-09-07) while this seat was proposing to order two hypotheses on the banner's absence from a capture. **Its absence from a serial log carries ZERO information about the image.** This governs every video gate in this lane: CLAUDE.md's *"the run MUST show `wc` in the `⚡ kernel features:` banner"* is a check against the BUILD output, not against `target/serial*.log`, and a gate that reports `wc-banner=N` is reporting a build-log count. The wire-side question is answered by `strings` on the artifact, which is the same paragraph's other half — *verified reachable (`strings`), not merely compiled (banner)* — and the two halves read two different files. **And `esp-jetson` prints TWO of them, four features apart (orin 20, 2026-09-07): the generic banner is computed BEFORE the jetson force block and lacks `tegra,tegrasmp,bsptick,bsprun`; only `⚡ kernel features (jetson):` describes the card.** Quoting the first is quoting a pre-force snapshot — two ways to be wrong about one artifact, both of them build-log lines. `grep -n "kernel features" unaos/arroyo` and the same pattern over `unaos/crates/kernel/src` (line numbers drift by branch; the three emitters are the fact)
- **A missing `build.rs` proves NOTHING about env tracking — ask the dep-info file.** `crates/kernel` has no `build.rs`, yet `target/aarch64-unaos/release/deps/unaos_kernel-*.d` carries `env-dep:UNAOS_FBW` and `env-dep:UNAOS_FBH`: rustc emits an `env-dep:` entry for every `env!`/`option_env!` READ IN COMPILED SOURCE and cargo checks them for freshness, so `UNAOS_FBW=1920 UNAOS_FBH=1200 ./arroyo kernel8-test` does force a rebuild and does reach the bench blit path. Raised by orin 18 as a refutation of their own audit's headline finding. **Two of their supporting numbers do NOT reproduce here and both understate the surface: kernel src reads TWELVE distinct `UNAOS_*` knobs through `option_env!`** (DMAWIN, FBH, FBW, GIT_SHA, NET4, NET4_BUF1, NET4_DHCP_MS, NET4_RINGDUMP, NOJB11, SMPPROBE, V3D81_SETTLE_MS, V3D_FIRSTKICK — `rtl8168_tegra.rs` alone reads five), **not one outside GIT_SHA; and of six kernel `.d` files sampled, two aarch64-base units carry both entries and two carry no `UNAOS_*` env-dep at all** (x86 units correctly carry none, `mailbox.rs` being aarch64-only). Not called a hole — the innocent readings are a check-metadata unit, a different feature set, or a stale `.d` — but the settling check has not been run: for each unit, which invocation produced it and whether `mailbox.rs` was in its compile set. `grep -rho 'option_env!("[A-Z0-9_]*")' unaos/crates/kernel/src --include=*.rs | grep -o 'UNAOS_[A-Z0-9_]*' | sort -u` and `grep -o 'env-dep:UNAOS_[A-Z0-9_]*' <unit>.d | sort -u`
- **`panel_info_nonblocking` HAS x86 callers** (`video/quarry/live.rs:1698/:2324`, armed by `UNAOS_QUARRY=1`); the "x86 has no non-blocking door" premise was false. (B11, the C1 provenance lesson)
- **`main.rs` contributes NO `panic::Location` to either default kernel image**, so a line shift there is free for byte identity at that config. Measured, not reasoned: `strings -n 6` on the objcopy'd flat images built at `0bb37470` with the default knob-off set finds 88 `.rs` path strings in the x86 image and **zero** from `src/main.rs` (same for aarch64-virt) — which is why the first GO-RED mutation for B17, a blank line at `main.rs:6`, was INERT and correctly reported byte-identical. `allocator.rs` carries one Location in each image and moved both. Scope: those two images at that feature set; arming features can pull main.rs code in, and this says nothing about `kernel8.img`. Independently corroborates the S7 step-1 x86-identity argument, which reasoned from cfg-gating — this measures the stronger fact. (B17)
- **Executor worktrees are cut from `main`, not the track tip** (→ P1). (rmbp-10)
- **ONE declaration, THREE correct line numbers in circulation in one evening — the lane rule's citation discipline, measured instead of argued.** orin 23 cited the zero-slot sentinel in `drivers/block.rs` at `:1479`/`:1677`, pi 9 at `:1492`/`:1689`, and both are right: orin's are the DOC-COMMENT lines, pi's the `slot_id: 0,` FIELD lines, so the same tree yields two numbers depending on which part of the declaration you point at. Across refs, `origin/main`, `origin/hw-jetson`, `origin/hw-pi4` and `hw-rmbp` all read `:1492`/`:1689` while `exec-orin22-bootroot` reads `:1518`/`:1715` — a uniform +26 from the lines BOOTROOT adds above. **Two independent axes of drift at once (which tree, and declaration-versus-comment), which is why a cross-tree citation naming neither is unfalsifiable. A THIRD axis turned up the same night on a file outside git — WHICH MOMENT, unresolvable by any command because a bench tool has no history to cite (B105).** Directly reproduces rmbp 16's own failure, where this seat "corrected" a peer's `arroyo:50` to `:54` inside a message about line numbers being fragile; this time the check ran first and the note went out as an explanation rather than a correction. `git show <ref>:unaos/crates/kernel/src/drivers/block.rs` piped to `grep -n 'slot_id: 0,'`, five refs
